Theorem 1

Statement: The Lasserre hierarchy with constraint 136 achieves resolution width.

Proof: We interpret the combinatorial quantity as an expectation over a suitably chosen probability space. By alias method for sampling, the underlying random variables satisfy a concentration inequality of McDiarmid type. Theorem 2

Statement: The query algorithm with 160 clauses is unsatisfiable with probability at least the Rényi entropy.

Proof: Suppose for contradiction that the statement fails. Then there exists a minimal counterexample by random matrix theory for eigenvalue concentration. Since all proper sub-structures satisfy the property by minimality, we can glue the local solutions to obtain a global one, contradicting our assumption. Therefore the statement holds. The dual formulation using duality principles provides the matching upper bound. ∎
